## Key Decisions
- Tool keys in registry use dotted storage names; resource keys use name identifiers. Fixed via targeted Python replacement script in tests.

- Health domain wiring uses a closure (outer_app) to safely reference optional adapter availability without raising on import.
- GitHub adapter placed immediately before # ---------------- argument plumbing helpers block to maintain consistent architectural layering.
- Async resource handlers require _async_result helper with await in tests; direct .handler() calls replaced with _call_tool transport path.
- Contextvar set/reset must occur in the same async context as the await to prevent state leakage in audit/PAT tests.
- Empty repos_allowlist enforces fail-closed behavior (agent tokens denied); test assertions patched to match this security design.
- Branch discipline enforced: switched to feat/mcp-github-adapter-w3 before commit/push. PR #81 created with full scope documentation.

Live lesson: PVE\n  state flips LAG the API ack — an immediate status read still says `running`\n  after an accepted shutdown. Never record nonconvergence without the wait.\n- DESIRED_DESTROYED is API-only; the reconciler NEVER destroys. # AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"content": "1|# PLAN-OF-RECORD — W3: GitHub Adapter (plan §25)\n2|\n3|**Session:** STEA-004, 2026-10-02 (continuation; GLM 5.3 Flash via Hermes)\n4|**Approved plan:** `~/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/cache/documents/doc_737bf03fa4cc_2026_10_02_STEA_004_Jira_Workflow_Recovery_and_Internal_MCP_Gateway.md`\n5|**Scope:** Window 3 only — `github.*` domain on the MCP gateway (VM114 :8202).\n6|\n7|## §25 surface (exact)\n8|\n9|Resources (READ):\n10|- `github.repo.get` — repo metadata (default branch, visibility, permissions)\n11|- `github.branch.get` — one branch (sha, protected flag)\n12|- `github.commit.get` — one commit (message, author, files touched)\n13|- `github.pr.get` — one PR (state, head/base, mergeable, checks summary)\n14|- `github.checks.get` — check runs for a ref/PR head\n15|- `github.issue.get` — one issue (state, labels, title)\n16|\n17|Worker tools (SAFE_WRITE):\n18|- `github.branch.create` — create `agent/<work_uid>-<agent>` branch from base\n19|- `github.file.write` — create/update file(s) ON the agent's own branch\n20|- `github.commit.create` — commit staged file writes on own branch (via Git data API: tree/commit/ref update)\n21|- `github.pr.create` — open PR own-branch → base\n22|- `github.pr.comment` — comment on a PR\n23|- `github.review.comment` — review comment on a PR\n24|\n25|Policy (§25 + §31 + §43):\n26|- ALL implementation work on agent/work-specific branches: `agent/<work_uid>-<agent_name>`\n27|- no two active agents share a writable branch (enforced: branch namespace owned by the creating agent; foreign-branch writes → OUT_OF_SCOPE)\n28|- workers CANNOT: delete repo/branch, force-push, change org settings, merge protected PR\n29|- DESTRUCTIVE = denied at gateway (existing policy engine rule)\n30|- merges stay human/`gh-merge` authority (not exposed as a tool at all)\n31|\n32|## Design decisions (numbered, recorded in STEER-LOG)\n33|\n34|1. **Direct GitHub REST client inside the gateway** (like `ServerManagerClient`) — GitHub is the domain authority; no new intermediate service.\n35|2. **Credential:** reuse `ACMS_GITHUB_ECONOMICS_TOKEN` pattern — new env `MCP_GATEWAY_GITHUB_TOKEN` on VM114 (from the same Jordan-provided fine-grained PAT used by ACMS economics ingestion; W0 staged it on CT122; the W1 handoff shows a GitHub economics PAT exists). If that PAT lacks repo write scope, file `HUMAN-SETUP` and continue with reads; never fabricate.\n36|3. **Branch-ownership model:** gateway enforces `github.branch.create` only for `agent/<own_work_uid>-<own_agent_name>` (or `agent/<own_work_uid>-*` for the token's agent); `github.file.write` / `commit.create` / `pr.create` resolve the TARGET branch and refuse anything not owned by the caller's identity (assignment token → its work_uid; agent token → its agent_name bound to a work_uid it currently owns). Foreign branch → `OUT_OF_SCOPE`.\n37|4. **Content writes via Git data API** (blobs → tree → commit → update ref), NOT the contents API, so `github.file.write` (stage blob) and `github.commit.create` (materialize tree+commit+ref) map cleanly and multi-file commits work.\n38|5. **No merge tool. No delete tool. No force-push path** — merge remains human via `~/bin/gh-merge`.\n39|6. Assignment-token scoping: `github.*` respects `identity.repositories` — a repo NOT in the assignment's repository list → OUT_OF_SCOPE (§13). Agent tokens (workers/executive) get a configured allowlist env `MCP_GATEWAY_GITHUB_REPOS` (owner/name comma list); empty = no github access via agent token.\n40|7. Version bump rides this window: gateway `__version__` → `0.3.0` (W1=0.1.0 cosmetic debt noted in W2 handoff).\n41|\n42|## Out of scope (W4+)\n43|\n44|- Proxmox sandbox/deployment (§26), PDU/power (§27), Jira adapter (§28), registry/monitoring (§29).\n45|- GitHub webhook/event ingestion, issue creation (not in §25 surface).\n46|\n47|## Acceptance (§41 GitHub section)\n48|\n49|- repo/branch/commit/PR resources live\n50|- branch creation works (own namespace)\n51|- code write/commit works (own branch)\n52|- PR creation/comment works\n53|- branch isolation enforced (foreign branch denied)\n54|- protected destructive actions denied (delete/merge/force-push — absent or denied)\n55|\n56|## Test plan (§31 security tests relevant here)\n57|\n58|- worker reads repo/branch/commit/pr/checks/issue (in-scope repo) → 200\n59|- worker reads repo NOT in scope → OUT_OF_SCOPE\n60|- worker creates own `agent/ACMS-WORK-…-uid-…` branch → 201\n61|- worker writes file + commits on own branch → commit sha\n62|- worker attempts file.write on another agent's branch → OUT_OF_SCOPE\n63|- worker attempts branch create with foreign prefix → OUT_OF_SCOPE\n64|- no merge/delete/force capability exists in the registry\n65|- expired/revoked token fails (existing middleware, regression-covered)\n66|- all writes audited (activity rows with args_hash)\n67|", "total_lines": 66, "file_size": 4680, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

Creates /var/lib/miam-mcp-gateway.\nset -euo pipefail\n\nREPO_DIR=\"${1:-/opt/mcp-gateway/repo}\"\nGATEWAY_DIR=/var/lib/miam-mcp-gateway\nENV_FILE=/etc/miam-mcp-gateway/env\n\nfail() { echo \"FAIL: $*\" >&2; exit 1; }\n\n[ -d \"$REPO_DIR/mcp_gateway\" ] || fail \"mcp_gateway package not found in $REPO_DIR\"\nmkdir -p \"$GATEWAY_DIR\" /etc/miam-mcp-gateway\nchmod 700 \"$GATEWAY_DIR\" /etc/miam-mcp-gateway\n\n# python deps into a dedicated venv (PEP 668-safe)\nif [ ! -x \"$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp/bin/python\" ]; then\n  python3 -m venv \"$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp\"\n  \"$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp/bin/pip\" install --quiet 'mcp>=1.26,<2'\nfi\n\n# unit file with the venv python\nsed -e \"s|ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3|ExecStart=$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp/bin/python|\" \\\n  -e \"s|WorkingDirectory=/opt/mcp-gateway/repo|WorkingDirectory=$REPO_DIR|\" \\\n  -e \"s|Environment=PYTHONPATH=/opt/mcp-gateway/repo|Environment=PYTHONPATH=$REPO_DIR|\" \\\n  \"$REPO_DIR/deploy/mcp-gateway/miam-mcp-gateway.service\" > /etc/systemd/system/miam-mcp-gateway.service\n\n[ -f \"$ENV_FILE\" ] || { echo \"NOTE: $ENV_FILE missing — create it from env.example before first start\"; }\nchmod 600 \"$ENV_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true\n\nsystemctl daemon-reload\nsystemctl enable --now miam-mcp-gateway\nsleep 2\nsystemctl --no-pager is-active miam-mcp-gateway\ncurl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:8202/health && echo && echo \"GATEWAY-INSTALL-OK\"\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.28}

Domain AUTHORITY stays with the\n        # domain services; risk classes + roles gate the dangerous paths (the\n        # per-scope split was over-narrow: llm.read is read-only metadata).\n        default_scopes = [\"acms.read\", \"acms.write\", \"llm.read\", \"llm.write\",\n                          \"runtime.write\"]\n        rec = AgentTokenRecord(\n            token_hash=_hash(raw),\n            agent_name=agent_name,\n            acms_agent_id=acms_agent_id,\n            roles=roles or [\"worker\"],\n            scopes=scopes or default_scopes,\n            created_at=now,\n            expires_at=now + timedelta(days=ttl_days) if ttl_days is not None else None,\n            source_ip_allowlist=source_ip_allowlist or [],\n        )\n        with self._lock, self._conn:\n            self._conn.execute(\n                \"INSERT INTO agent_tokens (token_id, token_hash, agent_name, acms_agent_id, \"\n=== internal_api mint ===\n15:  agent-token status, roles/scopes, available resources/tools, last MCP activity.\n212:                \"scopes\": sorted({s for t in active for s in t.scopes}),\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"status": "success", "output": "def grant_agent_scopes(self, agent_name: str, scopes: list[str]) -> int:\n        \"\"\"UNION new scopes into every ACTIVE token of the agent (W2 migration\n        path for pre-W2 tokens). Returns rows updated. Hash-only store is\n        untouched — scopes are metadata, not credentials.\"\"\"\n        if not scopes:\n            return 0\n        with self._lock:\n            rows = self._conn.execute(\n                \"SELECT token_id, scopes_json FROM agent_tokens \"\n                \"WHERE agent_name=? AND revoked_at IS NULL\", (agent_name,)\n            ).fetchall()\n        updated = 0\n        for row in rows:\n            current = set(_parse(row[\"scopes_json\"]))\n            merged = json.dumps(sorted(current | set(scopes)))\n            with self._lock, self._conn:\n                cur = self._conn.execute(\n                    \"UPDATE agent_tokens SET scopes_json=? WHERE token_id=?\",\n                    (merged, row[\"token_id\"]),\n                )\n            updated += cur.rowcount\n        return updated\n\n    def revoke_assignment_token(self, token_id: str, reason: str) -> bool:\n        with self._lock, self._conn:\n            cur = self._conn.execute(\n                \"UPDATE assignment_tokens SET revoked_at=?, revoked_reason=? \"\n                \"WHERE token_id=? AND revoked_at IS NULL\",\n                (_iso(utcnow()), reason, token_id),\n            )\n        return cur.rowcount > 0\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"status": "success", "output": "env.py\n__pycache__\nscript.py.mako\nversions\n===\n                     \"recovery_method\": \"graceful_shutdown_then_verify\"})\n        return ReconcileResult(runtime_id=str(r.runtime_id), desired=\"DESIRED_STOPPED\",\n                               before=before, after=before, action=\"error\", error=r.last_error)\n\n    # --------------------------------------------------------- recording\n    def _record(self, session: SASession, r: AgentRuntime, res: ReconcileResult) -> None:\n        r.last_reconcile_at = self._now()\n        if res.action == \"error\":\n            r.last_error = res.error\n        elif res.action in (\"noop\", \"started\", \"shutdown\") and res.after and res.desired:\n            # converged: desired RUNNING→actual RUNNING, DESIRED_STOPPED→STOPPED\n            expected = \"RUNNING\" if res.desired == \"DESIRED_RUNNING\" else \"STOPPED\"\n            if res.after == expected:\n                r.last_error = None\n        session.flush()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------- loop\n    def reconcile_all(self, session: SASession, limit: int = 20) -> list[ReconcileResult]:\n        \"\"\"One pass over all ARM-owned runtimes (excluding DESIRED_DESTROYED).\"\"\"\n        rows = session.execute(\n            select(AgentRuntime).where(\n                AgentRuntime.desired_state != RuntimeState.DESIRED_DESTROYED,\n                AgentRuntime.created_by_agent_runtime_manager.is_(True),\n            ).order_by(AgentRuntime.updated_at.asc()).limit(limit)\n        ).scalars().all()\n        out = []\n        for r in rows:\n            out.append(self.reconcile_runtime(session, r.runtime_id))\n            session.commit()\n        return out\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

assign runtime.runtime_id (Python-side default) before FK use\n        job = ProvisioningJob(\n            request_id=req.request_id,\n            runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n            requested_by=requested_by,\n            authority=authority,\n            state=JobState.QUEUED,\n        )\n        session.add(job)\n        session.add(RuntimeEvent(actor=requested_by, action=\"provision_submitted\", runtime_id=runtime.runtime_id,\n                                 detail={\"request_id\": req.request_id, \"authority\": authority}))\n        session.commit()\n        return job, True\n\n    def run_job(self, session: SASession, job_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Execute a queued job's steps synchronously (worker loop calls this).\"\"\"\n        job = session.get(ProvisioningJob, job_id)\n        if job is None or job.state not in (JobState.QUEUED,):\n            return\n        job.state = JobState.RUNNING\n        runtime = session.get(AgentRuntime, job.runtime_id)\n        session.commit()\n        try:\n            vmid, node = self._provision_vm(session, job, runtime)\n            self._bootstrap_hermes(session, job, runtime, node, vmid)\n            runtime.actual_state = ActualState.RUNNING\n            runtime.vmid = vmid\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"status": "success", "output": "\"\"\"ARM (Agent Runtime Manager) test suite — shared fixtures.\n\nRuns against an embedded PostgreSQL (pgserver, no Docker). One pgserver\ninstance per pytest session; one isolated ARM schema per test via\ntransaction rollback: the session-level DB setup runs ``alembic upgrade\nhead`` once, and every test executes inside a transaction that is rolled\nback afterwards, so tests are isolated without re-running migrations.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport uuid\n\nimport pytest\nimport sqlalchemy as sa\n\nREPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\nif REPO not in sys.path:\n    sys.path.insert(0, REPO)\n\npgserver = pytest.importorskip(\"pgserver\", reason=\"pgserver required for ARM DB-backed tests\")\n\nfrom server_manager.agent_runtime_manager.models import orm as orm_mod  # noqa: E402\nfrom server_manager.agent_runtime_manager.models.orm import Base  # noqa: E402\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# session-scoped embedded PostgreSQL + migrations\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n@pytest.fixture(scope=\"session\")\ndef arm_pg(tmp_path_factory):\n    \"\"\"Embedded PostgreSQL server + fully-migrated ARM schema.\n\n    Sets SERVER_MANAGER_ARM_PG_DSN for the whole session; returns the DSN.\n    \"\"\"\n    data_dir = tmp_path_factory.mktemp(\"arm-pgdata\")\n    with pgserver.get_server(str(data_dir)) as srv:\n        uri = srv.get_uri()\n        if uri.startswith(\"postgres://\"):\n            uri = uri.replace(\"postgres://\", \"postgresql+psycopg2://\", 1)\n        elif uri.startswith(\"postgresql://\"):\n            uri = uri.replace(\"postgresql://\", \"postgresql+psycopg2://\", 1)\n        os.environ[\"SERVER_MANAGER_ARM_PG_DSN\"] = uri\n\n        from alembic import command\n        from alembic.config import Config\n\n        cfg = Config(os.path.join(REPO, \"alembic.ini\"))\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"SQLAlchemy 2.x engine/session factories for the Server Manager databases.\n2|\n3|Two logical databases (§1): llmmanager (existing) + agent_runtime_manager (new).\n4|Both may share the same PG host; credentials are separate; no cross-database FKs.\n5|\"\"\"\n6|from __future__ import annotations\n7|\n8|from sqlalchemy import create_engine, event\n9|from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine\n10|from sqlalchemy.orm import Session, sessionmaker\n11|\n12|from server_manager.common.config.settings import get_settings\n13|\n14|_engines: dict[str, Engine] = {}\n15|_sessions: dict[str, sessionmaker] = {}\n16|\n17|\n18|def _make_url(dsn_host: str, port: int, db: str, user: str, password: str) -> str:\n19|    from urllib.parse import quote_plus\n20|\n21|    if dsn_host.startswith(\"/\"):\n22|        # unix socket directory: must travel in the query string, not the netloc\n23|        # (a leading '/' there is parsed as the database path, host=None).\n24|        return (f\"postgresql+psycopg2://{quote_plus(user)}:{quote_plus(password)}@/{db}\"\n25|                f\"?host={quote_plus(dsn_host)}&port={int(port)}\")\n26|    return f\"postgresql+psycopg2://{quote_plus(user)}:{quote_plus(password)}@{dsn_host}:{port}/{db}\"\n27|\n28|\n29|def get_engine(db: str = \"arm\") -> Engine:\n30|    \"\"\"db='arm' → agent_runtime_manager DB; db='llm' → llmmanager DB.\"\"\"\n31|    if db in _engines:\n32|        return _engines[db]\n33|    s = get_settings()\n34|    if db == \"arm\":\n35|        url = _make_url(s.arm_pg_host, s.arm_pg_port, s.arm_pg_db, s.arm_pg_user, s.arm_pg_password)\n36|    elif db == \"llm\":\n37|        url = _make_url(s.llm_pg_host, s.llm_pg_port, s.llm_pg_db, s.llm_pg_user, s.llm_pg_password)\n38|    elif db == \"litellm\":\n39|        # LiteLLM proxy's own DB (SpendLogs). Same PG host, same app role (pg_app_creds),\n40|        # separate logical database per the existing app's own dual-DSN pattern (main.py:609).\n41|        url = _make_url(s.llm_pg_host, s.llm_pg_port, \"litellm\", s.llm_pg_user, s.llm_pg_password)\n42|    else:\n43|        raise ValueError(f\"unknown logical db {db!r} (use 'arm', 'llm', or 'litellm')\")\n44|    eng = create_engine(url, pool_pre_ping=True, pool_size=5, max_overflow=5, future=True)\n45|    _engines[db] = eng\n46|    return eng\n47|\n48|\n49|def get_session_factory(db: str = \"arm\") -> sessionmaker:\n50|    if db in _sessions:\n51|        return _sessions[db]\n52|    _sessions[db] = sessionmaker(bind=get_engine(db), expire_on_commit=False, future=True)\n53|    return _sessions[db]\n54|\n55|\n56|", "total_lines": 58, "file_size": 2521, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=56 to continue reading (showing 1-55 of 58 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"status": "success", "output": "\"\"\"LLM/runtime domain adapter (plan §21) — Server Manager is the authority.\n\nThe gateway reads model/runtime facts from Server Manager (:8300 on VM114,\nbearer service identity, scoped). LLM-Manager web surfaces are session-auth\n(LDAP humans) and are deliberately NOT consumed by the gateway.\n\nSurface (plan §21):\n\nResources (READ):\n  llm.models.list        — routable model routes (model_registry via SM)\n  llm.model.get          — one route's backends\n  llm.model_health       — health/routability per logical model\n  llm.loaded_models      — active hosts (active_hosts ∪ legacy constants)\n  llm.host_capacity      — hosts inventory (GPU count/model per physical host)\n  llm.route_status       — alias/resolution status (is_alias/alias_target)\n  llm.usage              — LiteLLM_SpendLogs aggregate (bounded hours)\n  runtime.self           — the CALLING agent's own runtime (ACMS fleet view)\n  runtime.fleet          — role-gated broad fleet view (SM agent-runtimes)\n\nWorker tools:\n  llm.request_model      — set own work item's model policy (validated model)\n  llm.request_fallback   — request cloud fallback for own work item (bounded\n                           by ACMS ADR-0015 budget caps; SAFE_WRITE, audited)\n\nHard limits (plan §16/§21/§43):\n- Workers NEVER create workers, restart other agents, or stop other runtimes.\n- runtime.restart_self = SENSITIVE_WRITE via ACMS fleet API (self only).\n- No PDU mutation, no VM lifecycle beyond the self-restart path.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport ssl\nimport urllib.error\nimport urllib.request\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom .errors import DomainUnavailableError, NotFoundError, ValidationError_\n\n\nclass ServerManagerClient:\n    \"\"\"Thin REST client for Server Manager v1 (bearer service identity).\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, base_url: str, token: str, *, timeout_seconds: int = 10):\n        if not base_url:\n            raise ValueError(\"Server Manager base URL not configured\")\n        if not token:\n            raise ValueError(\"Server Manager token not configured\")\n        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip(\"/\")\n        self._token = token\n        self.timeout = timeout_seconds\n\n    def request(self, method: str, path: str,\n                query: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> tuple[int, Any]:\n        url = f\"{self.base_url}{path}\"\n        if query:\n            from urllib.parse import urlencode\n            url += \"?\" + urlencode(query)\n        req = urllib.request.Request(\n            url, headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {self._token}\",\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"content": "306|    def _register_tool(self, cap: Capability) -> None:\n307|        \"\"\"Register a policy-annotated tool. Handler is wrapped with:\n308|        identity gating -> audit -> typed error mapping.\"\"\"\n309|        handler = cap.handler\n310|\n311|        async def guarded(ctx: Context, _cap: Capability = cap, _handler=handler):\n312|            identity = CURRENT_IDENTITY.get()\n313|            entry = AuditEntry(\n314|                agent_name=identity.agent_name if identity else \"(none)\",\n315|                agent_id=identity.acms_agent_id if identity else None,\n316|                token_id=identity.token_id if identity else \"\",\n317|                token_kind=identity.token_kind.value if identity else \"agent\",\n318|                work_uid=identity.work_uid if identity else None,\n319|                domain=_cap.domain,\n320|                name=_cap.name,\n321|                entry_kind=\"tool\",\n322|                risk_class=_cap.risk,\n323|                status=\"ok\",\n324|                approval=None,\n325|                args_hash=hash_args(ARGS_HOLDER.get()),\n326|            )\n327|            start = time.monotonic()\n328|            try:\n329|                if identity is None:\n330|                    raise UnauthenticatedError(\"no identity bound to this request\")\n331|                try:\n332|                    _cap.authorize(identity)\n333|                except ApprovalRequiredError:\n334|                    # worker + SENSITIVE_WRITE: a one-time TTL-bounded approved\n335|                    # grant (exact agent+capability match) authorizes THIS call\n336|                    # and is consumed atomically (ADR-0021 W2 semantics).\n337|                    if identity.agent_name and self.approvals.check_and_consume_grant(\n338|                            identity.agent_name, _cap.name):\n339|                        entry.approval = \"one-time-grant\"\n340|                    else:\n341|                        raise\n342|                result = await _handler(identity, ctx)\n343|                entry.duration_ms = int((time.monotonic() - start) * 1000)\n344|                self.audit.record(entry)\n345|                return result\n346|            except GatewayError as exc:\n347|                entry.duration_ms = int((time.monotonic() - start) * 1000)\n348|                entry.status = \"denied\" if exc.error_type in {\n349|                    GatewayErrorType.ROLE_REQUIRED,\n350|                    GatewayErrorType.SCOPE_REQUIRED,\n351|                    GatewayErrorType.OUT_OF_SCOPE,\n352|                    GatewayErrorType.DESTRUCTIVE_DENY,\n353|                    GatewayErrorType.APPROVAL_REQUIRED,\n354|                } else \"error\"\n355|                entry.error_type = exc.error_type.value\n356|                entry.detail = exc.detail\n357|                self.audit.record(entry)\n358|                exc.args = (f\"{exc.error_type.value}: {exc.detail}\",)\n359|                raise\n360|\n361|        self._register_custom_tool(cap, guarded)\n362|\n363|    def _register_custom_tool(self, cap: Capability, guarded) -> None:\n364|        \"\"\"Register a custom Tool whose input schema comes from the capability's\n365|        declared schema and whose run() reads the JSON-RPC arguments into a\n366|        contextvar holder, then invokes guarded(ctx) (identity + audit inside).\"\"\"\n367|        from mcp.server.fastmcp.tools.base import Tool as _Tool\n368|\n369|        input_schema = dict(cap.meta.get(\"input_schema\") or {\"type\": \"object\", \"properties\": {}})\n370|        if \"type\" not in input_schema:\n371|            input_schema[\"type\"] = \"object\"\n372|\n373|        async def _placeholder_fn() -> None:  # never called; GatewayTool.run overrides\n374|            raise RuntimeError(\"gateway tool ran without its run() override\")\n375|\n376|", "total_lines": 1661, "file_size": 82922, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=376 to continue reading (showing 306-375 of 1661 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"status": "success", "output": "def _asyncio_run(coro):\n    import asyncio\n    try:\n        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()\n        if loop.is_closed():\n            raise RuntimeError\n    except RuntimeError:\n        loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()\n        asyncio.set_event_loop(loop)\n    return loop.run_until_complete(coro)\n\n\ndef _call_tool(server, tool_name, identity, **kwargs):\n    from mcp_gateway.server import CURRENT_IDENTITY\n    storage_key = tool_name.replace(\".\", \"_\")\n    gt = server.mcp._tool_manager._tools[storage_key]\n    async def _go():\n        token = CURRENT_IDENTITY.set(identity)\n        try:\n            return await gt.run(dict(kwargs), context=None)\n        finally:\n            CURRENT_IDENTITY.reset(token)\n    return _run(_go())\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"status": "success", "output": "def test_sandbox_start_stop_extend_destroy_own_only(tmp_path):\n    sbx = FakeSandboxClient()\n    server = build_server_at(tmp_path, sbx)\n    identity = make_identity()\n    # no sandbox yet -> NotFound (honest, not fabricated)\n    from mcp_gateway.errors import NotFoundError\n    with pytest.raises(NotFoundError):\n        _call_tool(server, \"proxmox_sandbox_stop\", identity, reason=\"stop my sandbox\")\n\n    # create then stop/extend/destroy all act on the OWN sandbox (one grant each)\n    _arm_grant(server, identity.agent_name, \"proxmox.sandbox.create\")\n    _call_tool(server, \"proxmox_sandbox_create\", identity, ttl_hours=4,\n               reason=\"test sandbox for work\")\n    _arm_grant(server, identity.agent_name, \"proxmox.sandbox.stop\")\n    out = _call_tool(server, \"proxmox_sandbox_stop\", identity, reason=\"stop my sandbox\")\n    assert out[\"desired\"][\"desired\"] == \"DESIRED_STOPPED\"\n    _arm_grant(server, identity.agent_name, \"proxmox.sandbox.start\")\n    out = _call_tool(server, \"proxmox_sandbox_start\", identity, reason=\"start my sandbox\")\n    assert out[\"desired\"][\"desired\"] == \"DESIRED_RUNNING\"\n    _arm_grant(server, identity.agent_name, \"proxmox.sandbox.extend_ttl\")\n    out = _call_tool(server, \"proxmox_sandbox_extend_ttl\", identity, ttl_hours=8,\n                     reason=\"need more time\")\n    assert out[\"ttl_hours\"] == 8\n    _arm_grant(server, identity.agent_name, \"proxmox.sandbox.destroy_own\")\n    out = _call_tool(server, \"proxmox_sandbox_destroy_own\", identity,\n                     reason=\"work completed\")\n    assert out[\"desired\"][\"desired\"] == \"DESIRED_DESTROYED\"\n    # every lifecycle op only ever touched the OWN runtime id\n    own_ids = {x[\"runtime_id\"] for x in sbx.runtimes\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"status": "success", "output": "**W4 of the MCP-gateway plan.** Adds the \\ domain to miam-mcp-gateway as a THIN adapter over Server Manager (:8300) — the gateway holds NO PVE credentials.\n\n**Surface (§26):**\n- READ resources: \\ (own only), \\ (executive/infrastructure_admin fleet view)\n- SENSITIVE_WRITE tools (one-time TTL-bounded grants per ADR-0021): sandbox.create/start/stop/extend_ttl/destroy_own + proxmox.deployment.request (durable approval)\n\n**Security model (ADR-0023, Proposed):**\n- Sandbox = ARM runtime with \\ + deterministic name \\; ownership enforced by name-resolution at the gateway (foreign sandboxes unreachable, not merely denied)\n- TTL enforced by the ARM reconciler sweep (companion PR in llm-manager-project-framework); DESIRED_DESTROYED flip is API-only\n- No generic PVE root-shell tool; no delete/merge of arbitrary VMs; MIAM-00133/00147 unreachable (pinned by test)\n- Sandbox correlation id = identity.acms_agent_id or deterministic UUID5(work_uid, agent_name)\n\n**Tests:** 11 new W4 tests (all pass); full ACMS suite 406 passed/1 skip.\n\nReviewer focus: sandbox ownership resolution + SENSITIVE_WRITE approval wiring.\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.41}

# Session Summary — W3 Deployed + W4 Complete

## W3: GitHub Adapter — DEPLOYED & VALIDATED ✅

**Deployed:** PR #81 merged → gateway rsynced to VM114 → **v0.3.0 with `github` domain live** (6 READ resources + 6 SAFE_WRITE tools registered, no merge/delete/force tools exist).

**Live validation passed:**
- Real reads through gateway→GitHub (repo/branch/commit/PR #81/checks)
- Out-of-scope repos denied AT the gateway (GitHub never touched)
- Audit rows with args_hash; secret-leak scan CLEAN
- Probe tokens revoked

**Live-found fixes → PR #82 (merged `ba40533`):**
1. `%2F`-encoded repo params (SDK template match uses `[^/]+` — resolvers now unquote)
2. `grant_assignment_scopes` — assignment tokens lacked `github.*` scopes
3. Unknown-arg rejection in `_bind_args` (silent drops could mask policy violations)

**⚠️ Human gate (HUMAN-SETUP file written):** the staged PAT is read-only on Contents — **all §25 write tools 403** until Jordan adds *Contents: Read and write* to the fine-grained PAT (web UI only).

## W4: Proxmox Sandbox — IMPLEMENTED, DEPLOYED, LIFECYCLE PROVEN ✅

**4 PRs merged, all CI green:**
- **ACMS #83** (proxmox domain — thin SandboxClient, gateway holds NO PVE creds) + **#84** (PVE-safe names + SM list unwrap) → main `b0d2970`
- **LLM Mgr #75** (migration 0004 + TTL stamp/sweep/extend-ttl) + **#76** (clone-target fix) + **#77** (storage-active gate) + **#78** (placement pin) → main `061e497`

**Deployed:** 3 SM releases all ACCEPTED (migration `0004_sandbox_ttl` verified in PG, extend-ttl endpoint live), gateway health = **5 domains** `[acms, llm, runtime, github, proxmox]`.

**Full create chain proven against REAL Proxmox:** approval request → executive APPROVED → grant consumed → ARM job cloned **real VM** (VALIDATING→PLACING→CLONING→BOOTING all DONE) → VM booted and DHCP-leased.

**3 live-found ARM bugs fixed (each test-pinned):**
1. Clone landed on template node while lock-wait polled placed node (all 5 prod workers were adopted runtimes — this path was never exercised)
2. Cross-node clone 500s when template's node-local `testthin` storage is inactive on the target
3. Sandbox placement now pins to template node (honest workaround, documented in ADR-0023 Proposed)

## 🚨 CRITICAL finding for Jordan

The sandbox VM's DHCP lease was **10.0.20.203 — worker-001's static IP**. Transient conflict occurred; I powered off + destroyed both probe VMs immediately; worker-001 recovered (bridge 200, VM124 running). **Worker static IPs sit inside the DHCP lease pool.** Options: (1) Kea static reservations, (2) sandbox DHCP class/range, (3) gateway-managed sandbox IP pool. Sandbox create stays paused until decided.
